The classes are priced differently because they have measured differently: over the sixty-one completed items carrying both an estimate and an actual, the forty-six whose authority is inside this repository ran at 0.71, and the fifteen whose authority is outside it split, the twelve that read, pin, install or measure an external thing running at 0.76 and the three that authored against an external authority at 1.42. **The 1.42 every open class-X cell is priced against is that second pool and not the class**, which is the whole of what makes n = 3 the weakness the risks table states. The fit's pools are recorded rather than recoverable, because a completed item carries its actual instead of its class, the class having done its work at the estimate: [the calibration record](#calibration-record) carries every completed attended item with its pool and the earliest estimate its cell recorded, and `tools/check.py` recomputes each ratio and count above from that record and the actuals beside it, rewriting them under `--fix`.
* **A completed item marked `agent-parallel` ran under fan-out rather than at one attention, and its actual is measured on a different clock.** When available, its actual is the summed agent-session wall-clock of the authoring, verification and repair passes the item took, which is not the attended figure the completed items before it were priced in. **The two series are not pooled**, and the ratio above is over the attended one alone. What the label marks is the execution mode and never the quality bar: an agent-parallel item clears the same gates, declares the same tier, and is read the same way. Unmeasured items retain this execution-mode label but supply no actual to the fit. **The series is ninety items** (S6a, S19, S20, S21, S22, S26, S27, S28, S29, S30, S31a, S31b, S31c, S31d, Q1, Q2a, Q2c, Q3a, Q4a, Q5a, Q11, Q12, Q13, Q14, Q15, Q16, Q17, Q18, Q19a, Q19b, Q20a, Q21a, Q21b, Q22a, Q22b, Q22c, Q22d, Q22f, Q22g, Q23a, Q23b, Q23c, Q23e, Q23f, Q24a, Q25a, Q25b, Q25c, Q25d, Q26, Q27a, Q27b, Q27c, Q30a, Q32a, Q34a, M0.20, M1.2a, M1.2b, M1.2g-i, M1.2g-ii, M1.2c, M1.2d-i, M1.2d-ii, M1.2e-i, M1.2e-ii, M1.6, M3.2, M3.4a, M3.4c-ii, M3.4d, M3.4b, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M5.3a, M5.3b, M5.3c, M5.5, M6.0b, M6.2a, M6.2b-i, M6.3a, M6.4, M6.5a, R4, Post-M10, I15, I17), the count and the list being the tool's, and **the re-fit across both modes is S19's, whose ruling is that they are not pooled**: the two figures are different quantities, no item has been measured on both clocks, and nothing in this tree converts between them, so the parallel series is fitted over [a record of its own](#the-agent-parallel-series) and stated beside the attended fit rather than folded into it.
* **A fan-out lane returns focused evidence; the integrator owns repair and the complete gate for a stable integration batch.** Follow [the tool guide's check schedule](../../tools/README.md#check-scheduling-during-fan-out): batch document edits before a checker run, target changed rules and tool behavior, and report drift and deferred checks without each lane launching bare `run.py`, full selftests or `--fix`. The integrator settles the batch's authored inputs, repairs the derived figures and runs one complete required wave over the integrated tree. A known-red checker baseline postpones selftesting; it supplies no mutation figure. Handoff remains provisional until the batch meets every landing condition, including the full mutation verdict where the tier requires it; lane evidence names its tested inputs and is never presented as the integrated verdict.
* **A count in a note carries the predicate it was taken with, and a figure the revision it was taken at.** Two readers counting one document at one revision can both be right and disagree, a case-sensitive pattern and a strict bullet head giving different totals, so a count is reproducible only with its predicate beside it; and a figure read off a working tree that a peer's push can move between two commands is reproducible only with its hash. F-208 is the class booked once, a search's result stated as a smaller set than the search returns.
* **The width test is set and it is a span floor: a class-X range's upper end is at least twice its lower end.** That is the *roughly a factor of two* this document already claims of every range, made a gate for the one class whose outturn says it is not decoration, and `tools/check.py` holds it. Class I carries no floor, having run under estimate.
  **The obvious threshold was tried first and is arithmetically unavailable, which is why this one is a span rather than a multiple of the midpoint.** *The calibrated value lies inside the range*, upper ≥ 1.68 × midpoint, is the test the ratio actually motivates; but a midpoint is the mean of the range ends, so that test is equivalent to lower ≤ 0.32 × midpoint and forces every class-X range to a span above five. Applied to the cells as they stand it would either drive the lower ends to a third of their midpoints or, holding the lower ends, re-price the midpoints upward by more than a factor of two, which is exactly the re-price the ratio is stated **not** to be. The two rules collide, and the span floor is what survives the collision: it is tied to the convention this document already states, it discriminates the class the measurement discriminates, and it leaves the calibrated total where it belongs, stated separately rather than folded in.
  **What the floor does not do is make the calibration a gate.** A cell can clear the span and still have a midpoint the ratio says is low, and nothing here reports that; the calibrated total is where it shows, and the 1.42 the whole convention rests on is fitted over three completed items, a count the record carries and no span widens.
* **`Wasm-parallel` is a legacy label on completed work, not a required execution route.** Gallina authoring needs its reviewed contract and proof environment, not a target compiler or image. Use `run.py proofs` and the applicable generated campaigns; use the Wasm oracle where the item's comparison explicitly requires it. M1.5 supplies the demonstration loop and M4.3 the staged IPC client. Another staging effort belongs to its consumer and is never silently required of every proof artifact.
* **Proof acceptance uses the content-validated incremental gate.** Run `python tools/run.py proofs` for a batch that changes proof inputs; its successful receipt covers the complete current corpus, including unchanged modules whose previous kernel verdicts it validates and reuses. A completed checklist item does not itself require `proofs --fresh`. Share evidence across items with identical proof inputs, and retain each item's required semantic review and generated campaigns. The [proof evidence contract](../../tools/README.md#current-evidence-and-generated-documentation) owns cache invalidation and full-recheck conditions; `proofs status` checks current source/output freshness without re-probing the toolchain.

* **Landing is two-tier.** Tier A is a full attended read: any register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit; any moved co-read pair; any new normative claim; every decision item. Tier B is a spot read of the item's findings, admitted on four mechanical conditions: checker green with every mutant killed, no Tier-A edit, the co-read ledger unmoved, and a rule holding whatever fact the item created.
* **A landing declares its tier, and a Tier-B landing names what holds it.** A landed item carries one `Landed: Tier A` or `Landed: Tier B under` line in its completion-log note, the second naming in bold every rule that holds a fact the landing created. **A bolded rule id is a holder citation and a plain one is not**: a rule relied on is written in bold beside what it holds, which is the form the landed items that created one already use, and a rule merely discussed stays plain, so the document tells the two apart instead of leaving it to the reader. A holder need not be new, an existing rule whose subject a landing extends holding the new member and being named the same way. K-84 holds every holder citation to naming a rule [the registry](../../tools/check-rules.md) carries and every Tier-B declaration to naming one, and it reads a declaration where it is made rather than demanding one where it is absent, a tier being a property of the reading a landing was given and not something a later hand assigns to it. What no rule decides is whether the named rule holds *that* fact, which is the residue every conferral here declares.
* **An item's acceptance predicate is authored and landed before its implementation lane opens.** M0.11 and M1.8a are the precedent and M1.8a states the reason: an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria has graded its own homework. In a multi-lane operation this is what lets a lane self-check, and it is the precondition for a Tier-B landing. **The five open items that owed one carry it**, authored at S18 rather than left for a lane to discover: M1.7, M4.4 and M5.3, which are on the critical chain and where the precondition is worth the most, and M6.3b and M6.5b behind the M8a gate. Where a predicate is not yet writable the cell says so and names what owes it, as M3.6b does and as three of those five now do over one of their halves, rather than staying silent. An item entered after this convention carries its predicate at entry, and one whose predicate is refused carries the refusal in the same cell.
* **Validation is generated, not authored, wherever an oracle exists.** The model-as-oracle technique is proven in this tree at 21,546 vectors (M2.1) and 658,659 vectors with twelve seeded defects killed (R1a). A hand-written corpus member where a generator would serve is a finding.
* **A fact has one owner and every other statement of it is generated.** Where an artifact restates a fact the model, the register or a contract owns, it is emitted from that owner and regenerated in the checking act. A hand-written restatement held equal by a rule is the older discipline and is a finding under this one: the rule can only tell you the two disagree, after they already do.
* **Separate start dependencies from integration dependencies.** A dispatch note names **Start** (the exact contract or artifact needed to author it), **Owns** (its deliverable and write boundary), **Check** (its acceptance predicate and focused command where available), and **Join** (the producer outputs needed for integration). None of the four restates a bullet of its own cell: where the fact is already written there, the label points at it, under the one-owner rule above. Its **Execute** note states the reuse, work split or early rejection strategy; the shared procedure in [Sequencing](#sequencing) applies to every task. **The note belongs to the item a lane is dispatched against, which is a leaf, and two carriers are admissible**: a leaf carries its own, and a later milestone carries none, staying deliverable-level until entry. **A parent's note is not a third carrier and M1.2 is why.** That note allocates ownership across a staged subtree, its own **Owns** clause ordering the children and its **Check** clause sending each to its own predicate, while M1.2e carries a full note beside it whose **Start**, the representation from b and the landed slot plan, is not the parent's. What a parent supplies is the allocation; a child's **Start** and **Join** are the child's. **A dispatchable leaf with no note of its own is a gap rather than a carrier**, its readiness sitting in its **Execute** bullet and the prose around it, which state the strategy and the obligations without separating what starts the work from what its integration waits on. Nothing here names which leaves those are and no rule reads the four labels, so this paragraph is read rather than checked. A missing contract or acceptance predicate blocks the affected implementation, not unrelated siblings. Contract review may open immediately; the implementation follows its landed predicate. A fixture or abstract interface permits local checking but never substitutes for the real producer at the join.
* **Split by independently checkable output.** A shared type, schema or calling convention gets one owner and lands before consumers change it; consumers then work in separate modules against that contract. Within an existing estimate, named work packages may be assigned separately, but the parent stays open until all its acceptance clauses hold. Independent completion credit requires child cells re-priced at entry, with no estimate on their parent; merely exposing concurrency changes neither the estimate nor the evidence.
* **`after the M8a gate` is a priority deferral unless the item names an artifact that only that gate supplies.** Independent contract, host-logic and test-harness work may use spare capacity before M8a once its own start conditions hold. This does not move its delivery into M8a, add it to the boot roster, authorize a scope-cut proof, or override scalar-first and measured-need admission of new datapaths. Final freeze decisions and measurements still wait for their complete corpus and required inputs.
* **Every fan-out subagent performs all repository work in its own dedicated Git worktree**, including scouts, reviewers, document workers and nested subagents. Before dispatch, the parent uses the repository's worktree command to create a fresh lane or verifies a dedicated host-provisioned checkout against the intended input revision; the brief names its absolute path, branch or detached HEAD, base, owned files, focused checks and integrator. Each call explicitly targets that worktree, and the integrator alone writes the integration checkout. The [tool guide's isolation procedure](../../tools/README.md#worktree-isolation-during-fan-out) owns placement and lifecycle for every platform and model.
* `Parallel` means the item can proceed subject to its dispatch note rather than to its position in this document; each assigned subagent uses its own worktree and any build outputs belong to that worktree's lane.
* **`Parallel` is about the build lane and not about attention.** A linked worktree derives its own lane under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, iterate tree and SMT cache ([I7](#build-loop-instruments)), so two items that both write `model/` share no build tree, no log, and no emulator, and a build holds its own lane rather than the toolchain: a second `run.py model build` over a live one in the same lane is refused by name, while a sibling lane's runs beside it. **Lanes isolate files and build trees, not CPU.** The machine stays one thing, twelve cores under one WSL VM, so concurrent model work is priced in wall time rather than in correctness. A provenance decision, document act or checker rule can use its own worktree without starting a model build; its checks still share the machine's capacity.
* **One integrator owns shared landing files, the review queue and gate scheduling.** Implementation lanes return their commit identity, inputs, focused evidence, findings, deferred checks and proposed completion note; the integrator reconciles the checklist, completion log, generated arithmetic and any shared registration files. The dispatch note's **Check** names the lane's focused commands and the integrator's remaining gates. Reserve the Tier-A read before starting work that needs it, and prioritize contracts that release consumers and repairs on the M8a path. Keep the integrated checkout stable through validation, share its verdict across the batch, and rerun checks when their inputs change or a failure needs investigation. Separate worktrees do not make overlapping edits, CPU capacity or shared mutable toolchain installs independent. `run.py seed properties` has exclusive use of the checkout it mutates, including against readers and gate runs; schedule other work in a different checkout with isolated outputs.
* Later milestones remain deliverable-level until entry.

### Current summary

These figures sum the priced reference-build, assessment and named hardening cells. They are not a total for the end-to-end proof program. The full compiler/security-preservation, native refinement/admission, binary constant-time, WCET, storage crash/concurrency/liveness composition and RTL-to-Sail proofs are not fully costed here. Physical fabrication, laboratory calendar and additional qualification campaigns also lie outside these engineering cells. [The unassigned proof map](../assurance/unassigned-proof-map.md) prices the unassigned proof and specification slices, none of which is in these sums until its cell is placed; the existing hardware items keep their measured-device and laboratory boundaries. Completed actuals, retained planning weights and the separate execution clocks retain the conventions above.

The membership is the author's and the sum is arithmetic `tools/check.py` recomputes over those cells like the two gate figures above, so a re-price of anything on the chain moves it in the same edit, and it is read as a serial-path ceiling rather than as a bill.

### Sequencing

Dispatch by available inputs. The item-local **Start** and **Join** conditions own readiness; milestone numbers group acceptance evidence. Give contracts and repairs that release the compiler-to-boot path first claim on review and build capacity. Open an independent lane only when its write boundary, useful output and review slot are clear. A queue of finished-but-unreviewed work is a reason to finish integration before starting more.

With the fan-out having already run ninety agent-parallel landings, the remaining scheduling constraint is the inputs and review capacity each new lane needs.

| Work package | Start and overlap | Integration boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Compiler representation and consumer contracts | Consume M1.2b's accepted representation, assembly campaign and producer receipts; resolve the ABI choices listed in [purecap-abi.md](contracts/purecap-abi.md); use M1.2f's `run.py compiler-diff` driver for staged comparisons and the integrated backend | Reuse the landed representation and permission helper; g and e keep their separate remaining obligations; d joins their outputs and f accepts the integrated backend |
| Firmware, kernel and storage | M3.5 and M4.4 author against the agreed entry/ABI contracts while the compiler runs; M5.3 reviews recovery and block-device semantics and authors policy-independent logic beside them | Each consumes the accepted backend and the real producer handoff its predicate names; M7.1 joins executable components |
| Composition and measurement | M7.1 settles the roster and assigns lowering of statement-only members early; M7.2/M7.3 prepare instrumentation together | Both analyses consume the same accepted roster, sharing captures where their workloads match; M8a consumes their distinct verdicts |
| Scalar RTL and platform | R1b curates datapath modules; R1c-ii prepares independent device wrappers against reviewed ports; R2 prepares trace/BMC harnesses; R3 prepares image loading | Whole-SoC and corpus checks use real core/device outputs; R3 joins M7.1's image; M8b need not wait for M7.2/M7.3 |
| Long-lead physical and board work | R5 prepares specimen/provenance protocols, M9a prepares activation analysis, M3.6b prepares timing observation, and M9 prepares board constraints | Physical qualification consumes measured parts and composition inputs; FPGA boot consumes R3. Modeled constants suffice only for the co-simulation claims |
| Bounded assessment and deferred host work | Use spare capacity for the open task's smallest decision or experiment, including independent document acts and contract/harness preparation | A decision or prototype releases only its named consumers; scope-cut proofs and new datapaths keep their own entry gates |

**Within every task, work in this order.** Identify the existing artifact and the unmet acceptance clause; resume partial work from its recorded revision. Review the narrow interface and a decisive positive/refusal case before implementation. Run the cheapest check that can reject the approach, then build one complete case before expanding over a generated population. Stop an optional experiment when its predeclared rejection criterion holds; price missing mandatory work at its owner rather than absorbing it or dropping the obligation. Reuse producer evidence only for the same source, configuration, assumptions and output identity. Finish with the task's required join and the integrator's landing wave.

**Shared work is paid and performed once.** M1.2f owns the compiler differential driver; component tasks add cases. M5.3 owns the modeled block-device contract, which R1c-ii also consumes. M7.1 owns the software image and boot recipe, R3 the RTL comparison, and Q10 the additional product judgment. Q2c owns missing-foundation assignments, Q21b the alternative-route comparison, and Q19c/Q20b share one held-out adaptation. Consumers return a defect to its producer instead of creating a parallel repair or replacement.

The summary's serial-path ceiling remains an accounting bound over its named cells. Exposing overlap changes neither estimates nor completion credit, and dividing effort by agent count is not a calendar forecast.

### Risks to the sequencing

What would falsify the order above, and what each risk is answered by. Nothing here is a hedge: every row names either the item that measures it or the property that bounds what it costs. **Where the item has run, the row states its answer rather than the question**, including where the answer came back adverse or found the row's own premise false, which is the only way a risk table stops being a list of things once worried about.

| Risk | Severity | What answers it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The authority-class calibration rests on three completed items in the class that matters.** | High for the conclusion, low for the actions | Every act it motivates is independently justified. The convention already treats the ratio as a prior on range width rather than a re-price of midpoints, and the calibrated total is stated separately rather than folded in. **Who turns the prior into a measurement is answered, and the answer is adverse to the obvious repair**: S19 took the re-fit and ruled the two execution modes unpoolable, so the two class-X-authored items landed since, M6.0b and M6.3a, do not enter this pool, the count stays where it is, and what moves it is an attended class-X-authored landing rather than time. The re-fit also removed a reading this row had never claimed but is easily read into it: the class is not what makes an overrun, nine of the eleven attended overruns being class I, and what the pool of two says is how far over the two that authored ran. |
| **M1.1b forfeits SECOMP's integration path for robust preservation.** | High: that theorem is the §5 end-state | The arms and their forfeits are recorded at the decision. SECOMP's recomposition, blame and back-translation are stated over the compartment layer rather than over `cheririscV/`, so the pin stays for the front and middle end. Refusing the arm costs M1.2's re-price and no structure. |
| **M4.1b's read foreclosed the CHERI-seL4 start-from.** | Taken: M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a are re-priced upward | Answered rather than pending. S7 recorded the read, 733 of 1,045 tracked files carrying a GPL-2.0-only tag with the CHERI work contributed under it, in the 6 h its four passes took together; M4.1b took the *author instead* arm at 0.4 h; and all three re-prices the severity column forecasts are taken, at M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a. What is left exposed is the authoring itself, not an unread term. |
| **The M2 strike is wrong**, the multikernel wanting real SMP or the reference emulator being too slow. | High, for the whole of what was struck | Both rulings are taken: the multi-hart call at M4.4, and the throughput measurement at S4, which reads the emulator sufficient through M6 outright and past it against a boot budget M7.1 measures. Both upstreams stay gitlinks, so reinstating costs the pin. |
| **The Gallina front is authored against register acts that later move.** | Medium | Why S1 runs first. M4.2a is the precedent and the discipline: it authored what the register fixed and refused what it did not. |
| **Tier B admits a defect a full read would have caught.** | Medium | Tier B admits nothing without a rule holding whatever fact the item created, so the admissible defect class shrinks with every landing, and two of its four conditions are already gates that run, `run.py` deciding them on the machine the landing is taken on, while the absence of a Tier-A edit is a reading a person takes and K-84 holds the fourth as a declaration rather than as a promise. What an unattended runner would add is a second machine and a trigger, which S14 declined on a measurement. |
| **The model's own documentation bundle is not rich enough to supply what the four parsers read.** | Answered, and the answer is partial | S15 read it and both of the row's premises were false: the build had never produced the bundle on this host, and there were four live Sail parsers rather than five, `banks.py` having gone to the quarantine at S16. The bundle carries the definitions and the four parsers take them from it. **The retirement half does not follow and none of the seven rules retires**: each holds a figure sitting in hand-authored prose or in authored SystemVerilog, which a view of the Sail model is not a generator for, and that is a separate item at a separate estimate no cell carries. |
| **Generation makes a wrong fact consistent everywhere**, where a transcription would have shown a visible disagreement. | Medium | The tree's own evidence runs the other way. K-79's sites were held by nothing at all until the rule was written, and K-54's first run found a live disagreement in a document nobody had noticed. Consistency was never a property the transcriptions had; it was the property the rules were bolted on to create. |
| **The generator dividend does not materialize** across the Gallina front. | Measured, and adverse | The row named its own falsification condition and the condition was met: at M3.2, the first Gallina item to land with S13a and S13b in place, the QuickChick harness killed nothing and the enumerative vector grid killed nothing, all 244 kills being the prover's and the whole mutation score being the assumption gate refusing to compile. So the dividend is unrealized for a statement artifact, and what would change it is the widening M3.2 names and M4.3 has since performed once for the kernel surface: a `Require`, generator instances over the file's own types, and computable shadows of its deciders. It stays a small share of the reduction and is named per cell rather than asserted over the total. |
| **M6.2's offline-admission reading is wrong.** | Medium to high, and now only over M6.2b | S1 took the ruling: measured boot covers a statically composed image, which is what splits M6.2. The split is executed, M6.2a having landed the composition-time admission path at 12.4 h, so the residual risk stands over M6.2b alone, the on-device CIC kernel deferred behind the M8a gate, and a wrong reading costs that deferral rather than the work already done. |
| **The lanes exceed what one author can adjudicate**, Tier A being irreducible. | High for the calendar | Lane 1's queue is discharged, so the ceiling has moved off its serial content and onto the Tier-A read every landing still wants, which no instrument removes. The fan-out has run against that ceiling ninety times and the landings held. Admit another lane when its inputs are fixed, its write boundary is exclusive, and review and build capacity are reserved. A growing review queue calls for finishing or reviewing existing work before opening more; the serial-chain sum determines no fixed lane count. |
| **Rupicola's target does not exist**, which is the harder half of what this row covers. | Answered, and the answer forks the route | Both halves are now measured at the artifact rather than read from the paper, and both readings moved. The **subset** blockers are not inductive datatypes and general recursion, which are at zero sites and three nullary enumerations respectively, but higher-order code, at 73 of M6.1a's 100 definitional commands. The **target** is a fork rather than an absence: the route stands up whole and has two exits, and neither carries both properties this plan claims for it. The verified exit emits 39 words of RV32I with no capability instruction, which is the plain-RISC-V target R-18-002 forbids by name; the reaching exit runs through a pretty-printer carrying no theorem, so its admissibility rides M1.2 completing the backend. The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default. |
| **The Sail-to-µSail translator's terms sit in its packaging rather than in a licence file.** | Answered, and the row's own premise was false | The premise was that it carries no licence at all, which a search over licence filenames and the forge established of licence *files* and not of terms. `default.nix`, `nanosail.nix` and `monads.nix` each state `licenses.bsd2` over a derivation whose `src` is the tree itself, and their three `pname` values are exactly the three packages it ships, so the grant is permissive and total and the automated translation is available. What is absent is the notice a redistributor retains, which a vendoring act composes from `dune-project`'s own authors field, and the hand translation is the fallback it always was rather than the expected path. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the reading and the rule that governs the next one. |
| **Quarantine rots the instruments S16 sets aside.** | Low to medium | They move whole, with their selftest cases, and K-83 holds that nothing outside the quarantine imports or launches one of the sources it carries. **The two contract documents did not move**: they stay in `docs/`, what was quarantined being the machinery and never the obligation, with K-70 and K-55 still reading both from the landing loop. |
| **The product thesis is unproved where the security thesis is strong**: a correct refusal can still be a product failure, and the performance headline states more than its own rows establish. | High for the product, low for the security architecture | Answered on the authored half and open on the measured one. Q1 has split the headline into five claims each naming its comparator and its evidence and authored [the product-gate contract](contracts/product-gate.md), whose limits read columns no admission rule reads, so a candidate can fail the product gate while clearing every admission rule; every verdict there is undecided until an image exists, and its envelope values are proposed until the product's owner ratifies them. Q10 is the configuration at which useful behaviour, correspondence, resource accounting and physical feasibility have to meet, and it carries the scope revision if the laptop floor does not close on credible resources. The re-targeting from a handset to a laptop with an elastic desktop is the scope act this row anticipated, taken by register amendment ahead of Q10 rather than by it, and Q34 owns the work it opens. |

### Considered and rejected

Recorded so they are not re-proposed, which is the same discipline [the alternatives register](../background/architectural-alternatives.md) keeps for the design.

- **Presentation feedback exposing another surface's completion time.** R-15-236c already forbids the observation, including through one surface's present callback. The line-period deadline belongs to the scanout reservation (R-11-008, R-15-230); compositor occupants are permuted at major-frame boundaries (R-11-023), and their bound is against the next reserved scanout (R-11-022a). Q17 opens no implementation work for this declined feedback.

- **Lowering as a post-pass over stock `ccomp -S` output.** Wrong on this machine's own semantics: an integer write yields an **untagged** capability (M0.6e e3, R-05-136), so ordinary RV64 pointer arithmetic clears the tag on every address it touches. Rewriting each such site into a capability increment needs type information CompCert has erased by `Asm`, and CompCert deliberately conflates pointers and integers at 64 bits. The idea's residue is what M1.1b rests on: the delta is concentrated in `Asmgen` and the calling convention.
- **Cutting the rules, the mutation selftest or the co-read ledger to reduce review cost.** Backwards. That machinery is exactly what makes a Tier-B landing admissible, so shrinking it moves work back into the one lane that cannot be widened.
- **Adopting CHERIoT-Ibex for the main core.** Already correctly excluded, its distinct compressed encoding being what R-15-005's single-encoding requirement refuses.
- **Parallelizing the build further.** I5, I6 and I10 measured it: clang took the generated unit's compile down by half and the residual is Sail's own single-threaded emission, which I6 showed a compiler mode cannot reach. CPU is not the constraint. Hardware still helps, through S9's finding class rather than through throughput.
- **Deleting `model/dependencies/`.** Checked at S3's scoping; every member has a live consumer, softfloat serving the surviving vector floating point and `asio` being S11's socket layer.
- **Raising the attended-hours budget.** Not a proposal at all. It is the one input this document cannot supply.
- **A second fast emulator, a whole-system simulator, or a new proof language** without a demonstrated blocker in the existing workflow. S4 measured that no loop before M7.1 is emulator-bound, and the two-language discipline this plan opens with is what a third language would spend.
- **Shipping extracted host code and treating its native execution as evidence** for the capability, timing and hardware theorem. The host processor and its runtime become dependencies of a different assurance argument, which is Q10's reading of a host product.
- **Feeding generated integer-pointer C into the capability compiler and taking source-to-device correspondence as inherited.** M1.6 measured the reaching exit's printer carrying no theorem, so the relation at that boundary is authored or it is absent, which is the audit Q2 carries.
- **Replacing an unqualified cell with a simulator parameter and calling the memory qualified.** R-15-247m wants the measured macro with its overheads, and a constant in Sail can be the name of an unsolved analog system; Ramulator2 and OpenRAM enter Q6 as comparators and neither qualifies the proposed stack.
- **Adding up improvements that address one bottleneck, or crediting vectorization, quantization and synthesis the baseline already assumes.** Q1 and Q8 keep each alternative in an estimate scenario of its own, and the whole-program bound on a partial speedup is stated with the Q items.
- **Installing every candidate dependency now.** One experiment is selected, its dependencies are reviewed and pinned, and it answers a question before the toolset widens; Q6's two simulators and Q5's solver are conditional on the comparison and the enumeration that precede them, and the solver additionally on Q27a's disposition.
- **Adopting a certified constraint-programming or pseudo-Boolean optimization stack so that a planner returns a certified-optimal memory layout.** Decided by the register before any candidate's merits are weighed, and Q27a is where each rule is read against the candidates and each arm recorded. What is *not* refused is the pattern the placement work already uses, an untrusted search whose output the existing checks re-validate (R-05-066), which needs no certificate-checking import to run.

### S · Serial-path decisions and program instruments

The work that is undelegatable, the work that unblocks a lane, and the instruments the lanes run on. It sits ahead of the milestone track rather than inside one, because a milestone's items are the deliverable and these are the conditions under which more than one of them can be worked at once. Two rules sort it. **A decision is not an edit**: an item here that names an arm and its forfeits is closed by taking the arm, and nothing downstream is priced against it until it is. And **an instrument is authored before the lane that depends on it opens**, which is the acceptance-predicate convention above read forward rather than per item.

### Q · Assessment actions

The actions a reading of this design puts to the plan, carried as open items so that each is priced, sequenced and closed here rather than in a list beside it. [The critique's product assessment](../background/critique.md#the-two-theses) raises the first ten; the rest come from readings of the register against the outside world, and the provenance changes nothing about how an item is carried. Optional comparison items close by taking an arm, which may reject the candidate. Contract qualification can instead be an explicit input to an existing build item: Q22a supplies M4.4's revocation interface and may run beside compiler and firmware work. The individual dispatch clauses govern these dependencies; qualification of an interface does not claim its implementation or full proof. The later witness, attestation and physical claims wait for their own named evidence and are outside the M8a roster. Three readings sort the set. **Use more of the instruments already selected before selecting more.** Connecting a small generated implementation to the actual target, measuring a useful workload and checking the static resource assignment independently are the acts most likely to reduce effort without weakening the security architecture. **For capacity, start with less data and better placement, and change the physical topology only for a large physical gain.** Public immutable weights are the narrowest useful external-store experiment; more-transistor SRAM buys stability and voltage margin and not density; more matrix throughput does not lift a weight-bandwidth ceiling. **No figure in [the performance estimates](../performance/performance-estimates.md) rises because an item here is accepted.** Some of the repairs make those estimates less optimistic, and only a measured change against the current assumed baseline is credited, never a feature the estimates already assume.

**Assessment dispatch uses the remaining child and its unmet acceptance clauses.** Reuse landed contracts, instruments and review decisions; a completed setup or a reasoned rejection does not authorize commissioning its conditional implementation again.

Q14 and Q17 record the completed matrix and display/clipboard decisions. Q15 may start on the register and existing corpus. Q13's selection and host-build work needs no composed roster; its target performance comparison does. Q16 consumes the product limits, with proposed values still requiring the owner's decision. Q12 can read the candidate format before deciding whether another timing backend is worth qualifying.

Q19a and Q19b compare existing proof clients independently. Q2c and Q21a/Q21b supply the foundation ownership, core-design and compiler-comparison decisions; their reopening conditions still apply. The reviewed Q20a protocol is their common input. Q19c still needs checked concrete laws and target connections. Its qualified instance and frozen interface release Q20b's held-out adaptation; the same adaptation record completes both tasks' distinct judgments. Q20c adds final-artifact evidence after its producer inputs exist.

These trials add no prerequisite to the current M7.1 realization route. A failed candidate closes the corresponding decision where its predicate permits rejection; it does not block the boot path or silently retire a mandatory proof.

* [x] **Q1 · Establish the baseline and its rejection gates** · 1.5 h actual · 0.0% · agent-parallel
  * The headline is split into five claims each naming its comparator and its evidence, the product gate is authored as a contract with its limits, its record and its rejection predicates, every envelope value is proposed and none taken, and the README repair is verified at its sites. ([note](completion-log.md#q1-establish-the-baseline-and-its-rejection-gates))
* [ ] **Q2 · Demonstrate one single-source lowering route on a wire parser**
  * **Start from M1.6's fork and not from a fresh installation.** That item stood the Rupicola and Bedrock2 stack up in a switch of its own and measured its two exits: the verified exit reaches encoded RV32I with the proof intact, which R-18-002 forbids, and the reaching exit leaves the prover through a C printer carrying no theorem and then depends on M1.2's backend, which emitted plain RV64 on that program with its capability arms stubbed. The component is a bounded wire-parser-shaped one, the consumer M1.6 re-pointed the route at, with an error path and explicit buffer bounds, and measurement decides which parser.
  * **Generate the imperative output and its checked functional relation**, keep the output reproducible rather than edited, and make one small source change and regenerate to expose the maintenance cost.
  * **Audit the boundary from Bedrock2's word-and-byte memory to capability-bearing code**: pointer representation, provenance, bounds, calling convention, external calls and failure behaviour. An integer address is not turned into a capability by an unchecked change to the printer.
  * **Choose the device path explicitly**: capability-aware lowering through M1.2's backend, or a justified adaptation of the verified backend, and close the relation to the Sail model, the intermediate-language step included. A stock Bedrock2 RISC-V theorem is a theorem about another machine, and the Clight intermediate R-05-043 names is emitted by neither exit, so the route is a second front end until that bridge exists.
  * **Measure** generated code size, target cycles, peak storage, proof-build time and the hand-authored glue that remains, against the manual-refinement route, before the technique widens.
  * **Done when** one useful component has one authoritative operational source, a mechanically regenerated implementation, a checked relation at every claimed boundary, and an execution on the target model. A host-only prototype completes the first experiment and not the device proof. The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default until a comparison displaces it, and the item stops where the bridge or the backend costs more than the duplication it removes. Higher-order specification code does not fit the target subset, which M1.6 measured over the supervision tree, so nothing here is sold as compilation of the golden model; functional compilation establishes neither constant-time behaviour, resource bounds, robust preservation nor the hardware refinement; no general Gallina compiler and no verified collector is built first; the domain-specific generation already in the plan stays where it fits, and hand-refined code stays where it makes the implementation and the proof simpler.
  * **Ownership follows what a host can decide.** Q2a owns the host-only prototype; Q2b owns the device path that waits on M1.2f's backend. Q2c separately scopes and prices missing foundation work before it can enter the route, without expanding either implementation budget implicitly.

Missing primitive soundness, a compiler or artifact-validation theorem, and a TAL producer/checker or its soundness argument require actual checked prerequisites from separately priced owners before dependent work opens; naming an owner or completing its scoping act supplies no proof.

  * R-04-010b and R-12-085f admit an optional fixed-model module only through the complete module gates; no card, production theorem or measured benefit is credited by the design decision. The children below own bounded contract, admission, containment, circuit, physical and product work. Desktop and server qualification comes first; a smaller mobile module needs its own physical and workload evidence. No child gates M8a or supplies R-18-004a's first-release floor, and ordinary on-die and remote inference remain available under their existing rules. The estimates exclude fabrication, packaging development, a new proof kernel and full frontier-model circuit proofs; before a consumer needs any such missing artifact, its owner and separate estimate are entered here and its acceptance predicate lands. A prototype or an incomplete security proof cannot admit a production card. The complete interface, crypto, resource-bound and state-separation contract is the admission minimum; a whole-model functional claim additionally owes its complete graph proof under R-12-085l.

A total serializer and a total parser over byte lists are proved both ways, so each value has exactly one admissible encoding in R-05-051a's sense and a non-canonical length form is a decode failure rather than a normalization, with R-05-051b's no-slack rule carried by one pinned minimal digit form. Every maximum is a field of its bounds record, and refusals compute for malformed bytes, over-budget lengths, a trailing byte, a non-canonical length form, a subject naming another design or model, and an assumption outside the permitted list. The encoded-admission theorem answers R-12-085g's canonical half only: identities are opaque bounded numerals, so nothing here is content-addressed. `run.py proofs` enumerated the file's own 139 constants, by its per-module count over the file whose inventory digest the receipt records as `73316f81`, each closed under the global context and re-checked by rocqchk; its witness line demanded one record's witness because the gate reads only type-ascribed binders, and the FormatBounds and Certificate witnesses are supplied by authoring rather than demanded. No cryptography and no proof checking is implemented: certificate evidence is bytes the file bounds without inspecting, and the kernel receipt stays a host input beside the parsed evidence. The Narcissus derivation and its correspondence to this hand-authored codec stay with U-12's inventory and U-14, so R-05-042, R-05-046 and R-05-051a are not discharged by this file alone; the checked design-admission path, the cryptographic implementations, the session binding and the physical binding stay open, and a format theorem admits no card.
    * **Start:** Q24a's contract, Q24b's closure, the existing proof kernel and canonical parser obligations, and Q22c's session-binding qualification. **Owns:** the bounded read-only manifest and proof-certificate format, checked design-admission path, unit-key challenge-response model, manufacturing endorsement binding, fresh attachment/session epoch, and protocol-binding theorem against the qualified session model. Design admission verifies certificates under the existing kernel and records the accepted assumptions before composition; insertion authenticates an individual unit from the composition's finite admitted set against that cached design decision. A serial string, signature or accepted circuit proof alone grants no authority and supplies no fabrication theorem. **Check:** reject malformed and over-budget evidence, an added axiom, a different model/design, a copied identifier without its key, a foreign unit, replay, cross-session substitution, reset-induced nonce/ephemeral-key reuse and an endorsement for another circuit; extend the session-binding classifier and run its focused command. **Join:** Q24d consumes the admitted identity and epoch; Q24f supplies the physical binding evidence.
    * **Execute:** use the host's verified parsers and proof kernel, with certificates as data and fixed-function module authentication. A new contract, unit outside the admitted set, larger resource budget or different verifier requires ordinary composition. **Done when:** the acceptance predicate distinguishes proof validity, cryptographic identity, physical endorsement and owner authorization, and all rejected neighbors remain rejected through the attachment path.

**Evidence and cost stay with their owners.** Q2b owns the target connection, Q3b its functional/fault evidence, and Q20c promotion of the exact final artifact. Share source/configuration/image identities and baseline records across them, adding each task's distinct check. Changed semantics or bytes invalidate affected evidence. Each missing theorem, port, primitive or executable gets one implementation owner and one estimate; Q2c's foundation map is reused by Q19c, Q21b and Q25d.

Candidate selection is bounded by the current client's failed predicate. The [language strategy](../languages/verification-strategy.md#existing-tooling-and-implementation-owners) owns the proof-tool research map and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../../THIRD-PARTY.md) the incorporated/pinned dependency record. Reuse those readings at the same revision. Inspect new terms and dependency closure only at the selected candidate's incorporation; do not install every surveyed alternative. Model weights, generated output and physical-design inputs retain their own provenance requirements.

Q6's conventional-memory simulators are conditional on a topology comparison needing them, and Q19's automation on a demonstrated proof-client cost; what a qualified-source survey adds to Q19's candidate list is terms and not a cost, and the terms that decide whether a named candidate can be anything but a reading are stated at the child that reads it and recorded against its consumer by Q27c. **Q5's solver is conditional on both the enumeration's exposed need and Q27a's disposition**, which is the condition Q5's own cell carries and which Q27a settles by naming the solver shapes the register admits and those it refuses. A small decisive rejection ends an experiment before a new toolchain or subsystem is built. Mandatory obligations survive a rejected route and return to their named owners.

No assessment completion automatically increases a performance estimate. For an optimization affecting fraction `f` of runtime by factor `s`, the unchanged-cost bound is `1 / ((1 - f) + f / s)`; added transfers or changed scheduling require a fresh whole-workload comparison. Better packing is not more fabricated capacity, and host performance is not target throughput.

    * **Execute:** Reuse the implemented scratchpad. Prepare the measurement and denominator contract before either encoding branch; instantiate instructions only after the margin and profile decision admit them. A failed margin takes the specified deletion arm and avoids an unused opcode implementation.
    * **Dispatch. Start:** the implemented scratchpad and [matrix measurement contract](contracts/matrix-margin.md). **Owns:** the campaign plan and report checker before either instruction branch. **Check:** the contract's ratio, binding, completeness and refusal cases, followed by the host gate. **Join:** the accepted backend, composed inference workload, sustained target-time and energy evidence, and attended strongest-RVV review; no fixture closes the instruction half.
    * **Measurement instrument:** `run.py matrix-margin PLAN REPORT --json` checks the supplied plan and report bindings, complete case set, output identities, and exact throughput and per-watt ratios. `run.py test --only matrix_margin` exercises generated boundaries and refusal cases. The campaign plan and real producer receipts remain owed at the join; the checker neither authenticates measurements nor admits an instruction surface.
    * **Re-priced and deferred past M8a on this item's own finding.** If IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, so the cell prices a pin and a review rather than an encoding act; and the item gates no milestone here, its scratchpad half being landed and its instruction half conditioned on a margin no backend and no composed image can supply.
    * **The scratchpad half is in the tree and the item stays open on the other half, which the profile has not yet enumerated.** The software-managed scratchpad is an addressable region class ([sys/pma.sail](../../model/model/sys/pma.sail)): capability-governed plain memory at a fixed address range, on neither of main memory's two latency classes, coherence-exempt, and not executable, with a composition declaring a wrong one refused rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). The partition switch's single unconditional pass covers exactly the declared range and takes the validity tags with it (R-07-014c, R-15-007r, [extensions/platform/vmclear.sail](../../model/model/extensions/platform/vmclear.sail)); the attested devicetree carries the link from a class to its range rather than the range twice (R-09-007, [postlude/device_tree.sail](../../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and R-15-009's standing re-pin obligation is recorded beside the geometry the model declares for the unit ([core/core_class.sail](../../model/model/core/core_class.sail)). **No instruction is added, and what stops it is a gap in the enumeration rather than a shortfall in the work**: the frozen profile books the matrix extension as a *unit* and names no mnemonic, operand form, or encoding for it, and nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j).
    * **The custom-opcode ledger does not move.** M0.6g's three rows spend one opcode between them, M0.6h's four still do, and this item adds no fifth: custom-0 (`0b0001011`) carries `cclear` at `funct3` 000, `vmclear` at 001 and the indexed pair's width-coded 011 and 111, leaving 010, 100, 101 and 110 unspent in that opcode and custom-1 and custom-3 whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The matrix extension is not one of those rows *yet*, and which act admits it is now written down: R-15-014a's second-act delta carries it at **(ix)**, its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding together, conditioned on R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1. So custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole against it exactly as M0.6g and M0.6h left them whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits, and spending either here would be modelling a measurement-conditioned provisional row, which is the one thing M0.6g's exit condition forbids.
    * **What remains, and why it is a gap rather than a remainder.** R-15-118's coprocessor line and R-08-003's per-element checks need an instruction to be stated over, so they arrive with the surface and not before it; R-15-116's admission is already taken, dense int8/bf16 inference clearing the 8–10× bar and the extension kept, so what is unwritten is the encoding and not the decision. Closing it needs the profile to enumerate the extension's instructions the way it enumerates every other bespoke row's, which is a register and profile act ahead of a model one (R-15-001a).
    * **The margin the surface is conditioned on now has a normative denominator, and that is the one thing about this item that could still close it the other way.** R-15-116a fixes the RVV expression the margin is taken against as the strongest one the profile admits on the M class, dot products included wherever the standing adoption R-15-067l records has ratified by then, and requires the report to record the denominator's own extension set beside its figure. A batched dot product collects the reduction tree an int8 GEMM otherwise pays per output element and produces eight outputs at once, so it is a term in that denominator rather than in the numerator, and *the admission above was taken against a denominator this profile has since made stronger*. Both branches are live and this item closes on either. If the margin clears, the disposition is as written and the surface is a pin or an authored encoding. If it falls below the threshold, R-15-116 **deletes the extension by its own criterion**, and this item closes by striking its instruction half rather than by authoring one: `vmclear`'s empty matrix inventory (below) becomes empty by construction, R-15-117's software duty and R-15-009's re-pin obligation go with the unit, and custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for something else. Under §1's value function that is the cheaper outcome, which is why the measurement is worth taking before the second act rather than after it.
    * **The re-pin compares all three tracks.** R-15-009 names attached AME (`Ztt`), integrated IME (`Zvvm`) and vector-coupled VME (`Zvt`); [the profile's recorded readings](../hardware/isa-profile.md#3-custom-and-fork-and-frozen-instructions) identify their source revisions, and the [matrix comparison](../background/architectural-alternatives.md#x86-acearm-sme-architectural-state-not-imported) records their state and switch costs. The re-pin re-reads each candidate's plan and source revision, inventories registers and CSRs, complete zeroize scope, memory and restart behavior, dependence on M-class VLEN and the declared systolic geometry, and arithmetic, rounding and scaling semantics. No track is selected by its family name or vector-file reuse. R-15-116a's strongest-RVV-denominator measurement comes first: a failing margin deletes the extension, while a passing margin leaves R-15-014a (ix) to admit its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding through the existing review gate. Available independent oracle coverage is a term in that comparison, not a separate admission criterion.
    * Net change: 576 lines added and 22 removed across ten files, one of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **65 properties against 62**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys, unchanged**, the region class being a property of a list member rather than a key of the configuration tree; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, no configuration here declaring a scratchpad for it to carry; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
    * Ten findings.
      * **The profile books the matrix extension as a unit and names no instruction, so there is nothing here to admit.** Every other bespoke row in [isa-profile.md](../hardware/isa-profile.md) §3 names its instruction, operand form and encoding decision; the matrix row names a unit, a disposition and a re-pin target, and a construct the enumerations do not name is absent (R-15-007j). Three neighbouring decisions are taken and read as though they were this one: no architectural tile file, no block-scale register (R-15-117 keeping de-quantization in software), and outer-product sourcing recorded as an exploration question rather than an ISA commitment. All three are about what the extension does not **hold**, and a surface whose operands come from vector registers and memory needs none of it. What is left undecided is the mnemonic, the operand encoding and the opcode.
      * **The empty profile cell was the symptom and the missing sorting rule was the defect.** No Sail file carries a matrix instruction; what [core/core_class.sail](../../model/model/core/core_class.sail) holds is geometry, a composition declaring how large a systolic array is rather than a machine knowing what it executes. What sorts the surface is R-15-116's admission against a **sustained dense-GEMM margin**, a measurement against generated output, so the surface belongs to the second act at (ix) rather than to an amendment. R-15-014b now states the missing rule: a unit booked without an instruction surface names the act that will book it, and one booked with neither is a review-gate finding. **So this half is out of scope rather than blocked**, and modelling the surface now would be the measurement-conditioned provisional row M0.6g's exit condition refuses.
      * **A scratchpad is a placement rather than a structure, which decides how much of one a Sail model can carry.** R-15-167 makes it plain memory at a fixed range with no fill, no eviction, no valid or tag array, and no lookup that is not the ordinary address decode, so it is declared beside every other property the composition fixes about a range. That leaves one irreducible new fact, *whose* it is, everything else being a refusal over a field the PMA already had: not coherent, not executable, not on a latency class. R-15-165's own test makes the thinness correct rather than lossy, the contents being a function of an explicit software placement and never of access history.
      * **It is on neither latency class, and stating that relaxes an M0.14 invariant rather than adding a third class.** R-15-247's two classes are two media of *main* memory and R-15-168 makes the scratchpad the local tier above them, so the validator's *every array is on exactly one class* becomes *every array that is not a scratchpad*. A third `MemoryClass` arm would misstate R-15-247's own count, move the figures K-60 holds, and give `memory_class_at` a value no latency constant stands behind.
      * **The matrix half of `vmclear`'s inventory is empty, an enumeration result rather than a milestone's residue.** M0.6h booked two absent members; one lands and the other turns out to be nothing to clear, since R-15-117 keeps de-quantization in software, the tile file is not imported, and R-15-118's coprocessor line leaves the unit no state of its own. What a partition can dirty is the scratchpad and nothing else.
      * **The scratchpad has no size, and no configuration in this tree declares one.** R-15-108 selects scratchpad sizes and the exploration has not run, which is what `frozen` is false for in every class row; a range written into a shipped configuration ahead of it is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input. So the model carries the shape, refuses a wrong one, and carries no range.
      * **The scratchpad is unreachable from the C-class golden emulator, so this item adds no corpus member and its harness installs its own address map.** The frozen emulator is the C class (M0.10) and R-15-165 puts the scratchpads on the V and M datapaths, so no corpus program can address one. The three new `$[test]` properties install a region above every region the configurations declare and restore what they found: a property about a structure no shipped configuration has is a property that must supply it.
      * **A new per-region property does not move the key-set gate, and schema conformance catches it instead.** `memory.regions` is a *list*, so a member's properties are not keys of the configuration tree and both declarations agree at 270 before and after; what bites is the schema, which grew the property and refuses a region without it in either file. An unmoved key count is therefore not evidence that nothing was added to a configuration.
      * **The build tree is one tree, and a second worktree's build fails at `configure` rather than at compile.** `cmake` records the source directory in its cache, so a lane building the canonical build directory from a different checkout is refused with a cache mismatch before a line is compiled. The lever needed no tool change, [vos/env.py](../../tools/vos/env.py) already reading `VOS_BUILD_DIR` and `VOS_LOG_DIR`, and it is the mechanism by which two items that both write `model/` serialize.
      * **This item owes the checker no rule, because the fact it would hold is a model fact rather than a document one.** The one cross-artifact agreement it creates is between a class's declared `scratchpad_bytes` and the address map's declared range, both in the configuration the Sail validator reads, so the refusal fires on every run of every loop rather than on a document sweep. A checker rule over the same pair would be a second reader of one file.

### M1 · Toolchain spine (incl. the CHERI-CompCert prerequisite)

Build the *functional* CHERI-RISC-V CompCert backend as the first **target-execution prerequisite** (no purecap CertiCoq GC is needed, the on-device path is GC-free, §0). The host-side **CertiCoq → Wasm** oracle and component authoring proceed independently; the **GC-free on-device lowering** (CompCert-C/VST through CHERI-CompCert; arena extraction via MetaCoq→Rust onto the Rust→CHERI compiler for allocation-light components) joins the backend to produce runnable purecap artifacts from a trivial Gallina program on the M0 emulator.
Ship the frozen-dialect **assembler, linker, and image composer** (§0) in the same milestone; nothing downstream links without them. The bring-up realization of that sentence is M1.4′, the in-tree trio M0.12 already proved on the corpus, with M1.4's LLVM re-homing its hardening replacement behind the gate.
Build the profile-freeze analyzer (M1.8a, landed) and, behind the M8a gate, the backend provenance outputs and the ordered dictionary, outlining, operand-form, bitfield, stack-save, and indexed-address reports against the generated corpus: the freeze decides a profile nothing before the gate is compiled against, R-15-014a having made the provisional one a total compilation target.
Every later milestone is purecap and managed-runtime-free from here.
